1Use the following data:
Substance | ΔrH⊖ (500 K) / kJ mol−1 | S⊖ (500 K) / JK−1 mol−1
AB (g): 32 ; 222
A2(g): 6 ; 146
B2(g): X ; 280
One mole each of A2(g) and B2(g) are taken in a 1L closed flask and allowed to establish the equilibrium at 500K.
A2(g) + B2(g) ⇄ 2AB(g)
The value of x (in kJ mol−1) is ........... (Nearest integer)
(Given: log K=2.2 R=8.3 JK−1 mol−1) (JEE Main 2026-21 Jan-Shift-1, Level-7)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2026 · 21 Jan · MorningSolution ↗
Kp from thermodynamic dataOne mole each of A2(g) and B2(g) are taken in a 1 L closed flask and allowed to establish equilibrium🟢 Exact70
Concept Two independent routes to ΔG° of A2 + B2 ⇄ 2AB are set equal: the thermodynamic one, ΔH° − TΔS°, built from the tabulated enthalpies and entropies, and the equilibrium one, −2.303RT log K, from the given log K. That single equation fixes the only unknown, ΔrH° of B2, i.e. X. The whole trap is bookkeeping: carry the stoichiometric coefficients (2 for AB, −1 each for A2 and B2) and keep ΔS in J while ΔH and ΔG stay in kJ.
Key relation −2.303RT log K = ΔrH° − TΔrS° Same ΔG° evaluated two ways.
Solution
1. ΔrS° = 2(222) − 146 − 280 = 444 − 426 = 18 J K−1, so TΔS° = 500 × 0.018 = 9 kJ.
2. ΔrH° = 2(32) − 6 − X = 58 − X (kJ mol−1).
3. ΔG° = −2.303RT log K = −2.303 × 8.3 × 500 × 2.2 = −21026 J = −21.03 kJ.
4. Thermodynamic side: ΔG° = (58 − X) − 9 = 49 − X.
5. Equate: 49 − X = −21.03, so X = 49 + 21.03 = 70.0.
6. Nearest integer: X = 70.
 dS = 2(222)-146-280 = 18 J/K ; TdS = 9 kJ
 dH = 2(32)-6-X = 58 - X  (kJ)
 dG = -2.303(8.3)(500)(2.2) = -21.03 kJ
 -----------------------------------------
 49 - X = -21.03  ->  X = 70        <-- answer
⚡ JEE Trick Keep ΔS in J and ΔH in kJ apart: TΔS = 9 kJ, not 9000 kJ. Pure elements would be zero, but here all three are given explicitly — just use the stoichiometric 2, −1, −1.
Answer: X = 70.
2Dissociation of a gas A2 takes place according to the following chemical reactions. At equilibrium, the total pressure is 1 bar at 300K.
A2(g) ⇄ 2A(g)
The standard Gibbs energy of formation of the involved substances has been provided below:
Substance | ΔGf° / kJ mol−1
A2: −100.00
A: −50.832
The degree of dissociation of A2(g) is given by (x×10−2)1/2 where x = ______. (Nearest integer).
[Given : R = 8 J mol−1 K−1, log 2 = 0.3010, log 3 = 0.48] (JEE Main 2026-22 Jan-Shift-1, Level-7)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2026 · 22 Jan · MorningSolution ↗
Degree of dissociation from ΔGfDissociation of a gas A2 takes place according to the following chemical reaction. At equilibrium, t🟢 Exact33
Concept Get ΔG° from the formation energies, convert to Kp, then relate Kp to the degree of dissociation α for A2 ⇄ 2A at a total pressure of 1 bar. The given log 2 is the hint that Kp comes out a clean 2.
Key relation ΔG° = −2.303RT log Kp; Kp = 4α²P/(1 − α²) Ptotal = 1 bar, R = 8 J mol−1 K−1.
Solution
1. ΔG° = 2(−50.832) − (−100.00) = −101.664 + 100 = −1.664 kJ = −1664 J.
2. log Kp = −ΔG°/(2.303RT) = 1664/(2.303 × 8 × 300) = 1664/5527 = 0.301 = log 2, so Kp = 2.
3. For A2 ⇄ 2A: nA2 = 1 − α, nA = 2α, total = 1 + α.
4. Kp = 4α²P/(1 − α²) = 4α²/(1 − α²) = 2 (with P = 1).
5. 4α² = 2 − 2α² ⇒ 6α² = 2 ⇒ α² = 1/3 = 0.333.
6. α = (x×10−2)1/2 ⇒ x×10−2 = 0.333 ⇒ x = 33.3 ≈ 33.
 dG = 2(-50.832)-(-100) = -1.664 kJ
 log Kp = 1664/(2.303*8*300) = 0.301 -> Kp = 2
 4a^2/(1-a^2) = 2  ->  a^2 = 1/3
 -----------------------------------------
 x = 100 a^2 = 33.3  ->  33         <-- answer
⚡ JEE Trick Spotting 0.301 = log 2 gives Kp = 2 with no antilog work. For A2 ⇄ 2A at 1 bar the compact form 4α²/(1 − α²) = Kp saves the full ICE table.
Answer: x = 33.
3For the reaction, N2O4 ⇄ 2NO2, graph is plotted as shown below. Identify correct statements.
(A) Standard free energy change for the reaction is −5.40 kJ mol−1.
(B) As ΔG° in graph is positive, N2O4 will not dissociate into NO2 at all.
(C) Reverse reaction will go to completion.
(D) When 1 mole of N2O4 changes into equilibrium mixture, value of ΔG° = −0.84 kJ mol−1.
(E) When 2 mole of NO2, changes into equilibrium mixture, ΔG° for equilibrium mixture is −6.24 kJ mol−1.
(F) When 2 mole of NO2, changes into equilibrium mixture, ΔG° for equilibrium mixture is −6.24 kJ mol−1. (JEE Main 2026-21 Jan-Shift-1,Level-6)
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:
(a) D and E only   (b) C and E only   (c) A and D only   (d) B and C only
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2026 · 21 Jan · MorningSolution ↗
Gibbs energy vs extent graphFor the reaction N2O4(g) ⇌ 2NO2(g), a graph of Gibbs free... | JEE Main 2026 Chemical Thermodynamics🟢 ExactD and E only
Concept On the free-energy vs extent curve for N2O4 ⇄ 2NO2, the minimum is equilibrium. ΔG° is the gap between the two pure states; here pure 2NO2 (point B) sits above pure N2O4 (point A), so dissociation has a positive ΔG°. The small dips give the ΔG to reach equilibrium from each pure state.
Key relation ΔG° = G(2NO2) − G(N2O4) = +5.40 kJ; ΔGto eq = depth of the well B is 5.40 above A; the minimum is 0.84 below A.
Solution
1. B lies 5.40 kJ above the N2O4 level, so ΔG°(N2O4→2NO2) = +5.40 kJ. Statement (A) (−5.40) has the wrong sign → false.
2. (B) A positive ΔG° does not forbid dissociation; the minimum lies below A, so some NO2 always forms → false.
3. (C) The reverse reaction stops at the same minimum, not completion → false.
4. (D) From A down to the minimum is 0.84 kJ, so 1 mol N2O4 → equilibrium gives ΔG = −0.84 kJ → true.
5. (E) From B down to the minimum is 5.40 + 0.84 = 6.24 kJ, so 2 mol NO2 → equilibrium gives ΔG = −6.24 kJ → true.
6. Correct set = D and E (F is printed identically to E), which is option (a).
 B is 5.40 above A  -> dG0 = +5.40 (A: -5.40 NO)
 well depth from A  = 0.84  -> (D) -0.84  YES
 well depth from B  = 5.40 + 0.84 = 6.24
                    -> (E) -6.24        YES
 -----------------------------------------
 answer = D and E only = (a)        <-- answer
⚡ JEE Trick Every point on the curve slides downhill to the same minimum, so both pure states reach equilibrium with a negative ΔG. The sign of ΔG° is just which pure state is higher — here NO2, so +5.40.
Answer: (a) D and E only.
4For the following gas phase equilibrium reaction at constant temperature,
NH3(g) ⇄ 1⁄2N2(g) + 3⁄2H2(g)
If the total pressure is √3 atm and the pressure equilibrium constant (KP) is 9 atm, then the degree of dissociation is given as (x×10−2)1/2.
The value of x is ______ (Nearest integer) (JEE Main 2026-23 Jan-Shift-1, Level-6)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2026 · 23 Jan · MorningSolution ↗
Degree of dissociation of NH3For the following gas phase equilibrium reaction at constant temperature, NH3(g) ⇌ 1/2N2(g) + 3/2H2(g)🟢 Exact125 ⚠
Concept For NH3 ⇄ (1/2)N2 + (3/2)H2, write all mole fractions in terms of the degree of dissociation α, build Kp with the total pressure factor, set it to 9 and solve. A degree of dissociation can never exceed 1, which caps the possible answer.
Key relation Kp = [(α/2)1/2(3α/2)3/2/(1−α)]·(1+α)−1·P = 9α²/[4(1−α²)] Δn = 1, P = √3 so the pressure factor gives the 9.
Solution
1. Start 1 mol NH3: NH3 = 1−α, N2 = α/2, H2 = 3α/2, total = 1+α.
2. The mole-fraction coefficient (α/2)1/2(3α/2)3/2 = 3√3 · α²/4.
3. Kp = [3√3 α²/4] · P/[(1−α)(1+α)]; with P = √3, 3√3·√3 = 9, so Kp = 9α²/[4(1−α²)].
4. Set Kp = 9: α²/(1−α²) = 4 ⇒ 5α² = 4 ⇒ α² = 0.8.
5. α = (x×10−2)1/2 ⇒ x = 100α² = 80 (α = 0.894, valid). Back-check: α² = 0.8 gives Kp = 9.0. ✓
6. The key’s 125 implies α² = 1.25, α = 1.12 > 1 — impossible for a degree of dissociation.
 Kp = 9 a^2 / [4(1 - a^2)] = 9
 -> a^2/(1-a^2) = 4  ->  5 a^2 = 4
 -> a^2 = 0.8 ,  a = 0.894  (valid, <1)
 -----------------------------------------
 x = 100 a^2 = 80            <-- my working
 key 125 -> a = 1.12 > 1 (impossible)
⚡ JEE Trick Sanity-cap α ≤ 1, so x ≤ 100 here — any answer above 100 is non-physical. The pressure factor PΔn with Δn = 1 and P = √3 is what turns 3√3 into a clean 9.
Answer: 125 per the key — my working gives x = 80 ⚠
5Consider the general reaction given below at 400 K
The values of Kp and Kc are studied under the same condition of temperature but variation in x and y.
(i) Kp = 85.87 and Kc = 2.586 appropriate units
(ii) Kp = 0.862 and Kc = 28.62 appropriate units
The value of x and y in (i) and (ii) respectively are: (JEE Main 2026-23 Jan-Shift-1, Level-5)
(a) (i) 3, 1 ; (ii) 3, 1   (b) (i) 4, 1 ; (ii) 4, 1   (c) (i) 1, 3 ; (ii) 2, 1   (d) (i) 1, 2 ; (ii) 2, 1
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2026 · 23 Jan · MorningSolution ↗
Kp and Kc relation (delta n)The values of Kp and Kc are studied under the same condition of temperature but variation in x and y🟢 Exact(d) (i) 1,2 ; (ii) 2,1
Concept For a gas reaction the two equilibrium constants are tied by Kp = Kc(RT)Δn, where Δn is (moles of gaseous product) − (moles of gaseous reactant). For a general x A ⇌ y B this is Δn = y − x, so the measured ratio Kp/Kc pins down y − x. The trap is reading the ratio as the stoichiometry directly.
Key relation Kp/Kc = (RT)Δn, RT = 0.083 × 400 ≈ 33.2 Same T = 400 K for both, so RT is one fixed number; only Δn changes.
Solution
1. Write the general change as x A ⇌ y B, so Δn = y − x and Kp/Kc = (RT)y−x.
2. Set (i): Kp/Kc = 85.87 / 2.586 = 33.2 = (33.2)1, so Δn = +1, i.e. y − x = 1.
3. Set (ii): Kp/Kc = 0.862 / 28.62 = 0.0301 = (33.2)−1, so Δn = −1, i.e. y − x = −1.
4. Need a pair giving +1 for (i) and −1 for (ii). The two cases share the same T, so only x, y differ.
5. Test (d): (i) x=1, y=2 → y−x = +1 ✓ ; (ii) x=2, y=1 → y−x = −1 ✓.
6. Reject others: (a) 3,1 and (c) 1,3 give Δn = ±2; (b) 4,1 gives Δn = −3.
 RT = 0.083 x 400 = 33.2   (T = 400 K both)
 (i)  Kp/Kc = 85.87/2.586 = 33.2  = RT^(+1)
      -> dn = +1  -> y - x = 1
 (ii) Kp/Kc = 0.862/28.62 = 0.0301 = RT^(-1)
      -> dn = -1  -> y - x = -1
 (d): (i)1,2 -> +1 ;  (ii)2,1 -> -1   OK
⚡ JEE Trick Don't solve for RT precisely; just see that the two ratios are reciprocals, so the two Δn values are +1 and −1. Only option (d) delivers that opposite-sign pair with a single reactant and product.
Answer: (d) (i) 1,2 ; (ii) 2,1.
6Consider the equilibrium
CO(g) + 3H2(g) ⇄ CH4(g) + H2O(g)
If the pressure applied over the system increases by two fold at constant temperature then
(A) Concentration of reactants and products increases.
(B) Equilibrium will shift in forward direction.
(C) Equilibrium constant increases since concentration of products increases.
(D) Equilibrium constant remains unchanged as concentration of reactants and products remain same.
Choose the correct answer from the options given below : (JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan. Shift 2), Level-4)
(a) (A), (B) and (C) only   (b) (A) and (B) only   (c) (A), (B) and (D) only   (d) (B) and (C) only
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2025 · 29 Jan · EveningSolution ↗
Effect of pressure on equilibriumConsider the equilibrium CO(g) + 3H2(g) - CH4(g) + H2O(g). If the pressure applied over the system i🟢 Exact(b) (A) and (B) only
Concept Doubling the pressure means halving the volume at fixed T. That raises the concentration of every species at once, and Le Chatelier then shifts the position of equilibrium toward the side with fewer gas moles. The equilibrium constant itself depends only on temperature, so it never changes here.
Key relation Δngas = (1+1) − (1+3) = −2 < 0; Kc = f(T) only Fewer moles on the product side, so compression favours products.
Solution
1. CO + 3H2 ⇌ CH4 + H2O: 4 mol gas → 2 mol gas, so Δn = −2.
2. (A) Compressing to half the volume doubles every concentration instantly, so concentrations of reactants and products do increase. A is true.
3. (B) Q then falls below K (denominator grew more, having the higher power), so the reaction moves forward toward the 2-mole side. B is true.
4. (C) The equilibrium constant is fixed by T alone; it does not rise because products rose. C is false.
5. (D) K does stay unchanged, but its stated reason — concentrations remaining the same — is wrong, since they clearly changed. D is false as written.
6. Correct statements: (A) and (B) only. The valid fact about K (constant) is spoiled by a false reason in (D).
 CO + 3H2  ==  CH4 + H2O     4 gas -> 2 gas
 P x2 (V/2): all [ ] double      -> (A) true
 Q < K after compression -> forward -> (B) true
 K = f(T) only: no change        -> (C) false
 (D) K same BUT "conc. same" is wrong -> false
 correct = (A),(B)  = (b)
⚡ JEE Trick Pressure never moves K — only T does. (D) is the tempting trap: its conclusion (K unchanged) is right but its reasoning is false, so the statement as a whole is wrong. Judge a statement by its reason too.
Answer: (b) (A) and (B) only.
7A vessel at 1000 K contains CO2 with a pressure of 0.5 atm. Some of CO2 is converted into CO on addition of graphite. If total pressure at equilibrium is 0.8 atm, then Kp is : (JEE Main 2025 (22 Jan. Shift 1), Level-3)
(a) 1.8 atm   (b) 0.3 atm   (c) 3 atm   (d) 0.18 atm
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2025 · 22 Jan · MorningSolution ↗
Boudouard equilibrium KpA vessel at 1000 K contains CO2 with a pressure of 0.5 atm. Some of CO2 is converted into CO on addi🟢 Exact(a) 1.8 atm
Concept Graphite is a pure solid, so it drops out of Kp; only the gases CO2 and CO appear in the expression. The Boudouard reaction CO2 + C(s) ⇌ 2CO raises the mole count of gas (1 → 2), so the total pressure climbs as CO forms. The crucial trick is that the rise in total pressure tells you exactly how much CO2 has reacted, since one gas mole is gained per turnover.
Key relation Kp = PCO2 / PCO2 Solid carbon has activity 1; each CO2 lost makes 2 CO, so total pressure grows by the amount reacted.
Solution
1. Let x atm of CO2 react: CO2 = 0.5 − x, CO = 2x (C is solid, so it is omitted from pressures).
2. Total P = (0.5 − x) + 2x = 0.5 + x. Each reacted CO2 removes 1 gas mole but adds 2, a net of +1.
3. Given total = 0.8 atm: 0.5 + x = 0.8 → x = 0.3 atm reacted.
4. So PCO2 = 0.5 − 0.3 = 0.2 atm and PCO = 2 × 0.3 = 0.6 atm at equilibrium.
5. Kp = PCO2 / PCO2 = (0.6)2 / 0.2 = 0.36 / 0.2 = 1.8 atm.
6. Units: Δn = (2 − 1) = +1, so Kp carries one power of pressure — atm, matching option (a).
7. Re-check: the equilibrium pressures 0.2 + 0.6 = 0.8 atm sum correctly, confirming x = 0.3.
 CO2(g) + C(s)  ==  2 CO(g)
 start   0.5             0
 react   -x             +2x
 eq     0.5-x           2x
 total = 0.5 + x = 0.8  ->  x = 0.3
 P(CO2)=0.2   P(CO)=0.6
 Kp = 0.6^2 / 0.2 = 0.36/0.2 = 1.8 atm
⚡ JEE Trick The jump in total pressure (0.8 − 0.5 = 0.3) equals the CO2 consumed, because this reaction gains exactly one gas mole per turnover. Never put solid carbon into Kp — that would wrongly give 0.9.
Answer: (a) 1.8 atm.
8For a reaction, N2O5(g) → 2NO2(g) + 1⁄2O2(g) in a constant volume container, no products were present initially. The final pressure of the system when 50% of reaction gets completed is (JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan. Shift 1), Level-3)
(a) 5 times of initial pressure   (b) 5/2 times of initial pressure   (c) 7/2 times of initial pressure   (d) 7/4 times of initial pressure
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2025 · 24 Jan · MorningSolution ↗
Pressure change in decompositionJEE mains 2025 For a reaction, N2O5(g) to give 2NO2(g) + 1/2 O2(g) in a constant volume container, n🟢 Exact(d) 7/4 times of initial pressure
Concept At constant volume and temperature, pressure is proportional to total moles of gas. One mole of N2O5 decomposing makes 2 of NO2 plus (1/2) of O2, a net gain, so the total pressure rises as the reaction runs. "50% complete" means half the initial N2O5 is gone.
Key relation Ptotal / P0 = ntotal / n0 (V, T fixed) Ideal gas at fixed V, T: P tracks total moles exactly.
Solution
1. Take initial N2O5 pressure = P0, products zero.
2. 50% reacts: N2O5 consumed = 0.5 P0, remaining = 0.5 P0.
3. NO2 formed = 2 × 0.5 P0 = P0. 2 NO2 per N2O5.
4. O2 formed = (1/2) × 0.5 P0 = 0.25 P0.
5. Total = 0.5 P0 + P0 + 0.25 P0 = 1.75 P0.
6. So Pfinal / P0 = 1.75 = 7/4, i.e. 7/4 times the initial pressure.
7. Sanity: N2O5 went from P0 to 0.5 P0 while products added 1.25 P0, giving a net rise of 0.75 P0 — consistent with 1.75 P0.
 N2O5  ->  2 NO2  +  (1/2) O2    (V const)
 start  P0        0        0
 50%:  react 0.5P0
 N2O5 left = 0.5 P0
 NO2 = 2(0.5P0) = 1.0 P0
 O2  = 0.5(0.5P0) = 0.25 P0
 total = 0.5 + 1.0 + 0.25 = 1.75 P0 = 7/4 P0
⚡ JEE Trick Do it all in pressure units — at fixed V, T there is no need for moles or R. Distractor (c) 7/2 comes from forgetting the (1/2) on O2 (using O2 = P0); keep the half-coefficient.
Answer: (d) 7/4 times of initial pressure.
937.8 g N2O5 was taken in a 1 L reaction vessel and allowed to undergo the following reaction at 500 K
2 N2O5(g) ⇄ 2 N2O4(g) + O2(g). The total pressure at equilibrium was found to be 18.65 bar. Then, Kp = ______ ×10−2 [nearest integer] Assume N2O5 to behave ideally under these conditions. Given: R = 0.082 bar Lmol−1 K−1. (JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan. Shift 1), Level-3)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2025 · 24 Jan · MorningSolution ↗
Kp of N2O5 decompositionJEE Main-2025 | 37.8 g N2O5 was taken in a 1 L reaction vessel and..| Chemical Equilibrium🟢 Exact962
Concept Convert mass to moles, find the initial pressure from PV = nRT, then use the rise in total pressure to get the extent of reaction. 2 N2O5 ⇌ 2 N2O4 + O2 gains half a mole of gas per N2O5 decomposed, so each O2 made adds one net mole of gas.
Key relation Kp = PN2O42 · PO2 / PN2O52; Pi = niRT/V RT = 0.082 × 500 = 41 bar·L/mol, V = 1 L.
Solution
1. n(N2O5) = 37.8 / 108 = 0.35 mol (M = 108 g/mol).
2. Let x mol O2 form: N2O5 = 0.35 − 2x, N2O4 = 2x, O2 = x; total = 0.35 + x mol.
3. Ptotal = (0.35 + x)(41) = 18.65 → 0.35 + x = 0.455 → x = 0.105 mol.
4. n: N2O5 = 0.140, N2O4 = 0.210, O2 = 0.105 mol.
5. P = n × 41: PN2O5 = 5.75, PN2O4 = 8.60, PO2 = 4.30 bar (sum 18.65 ✓).
6. Kp = (8.60)2 × 4.30 / (5.75)2 = 318.0 / 33.06 = 9.62 bar.
7. As a multiple of 10−2: 9.62 = 962 × 10−2.
 n(N2O5)=37.8/108 = 0.35 mol ; RT = 0.082x500 = 41
 2N2O5 == 2N2O4 + O2   (let x = mol O2)
 n: 0.35-2x , 2x , x   total = 0.35 + x
 (0.35+x)41 = 18.65 -> x = 0.105
 P: 5.75 , 8.60 , 4.30 bar
 Kp = 8.60^2 x 4.30 / 5.75^2 = 9.62 = 962 x10^-2
⚡ JEE Trick Work in partial pressures straight from Pi = niRT/V so you never re-divide by V for Kp. Sanity check: the three partials must re-sum to 18.65 bar, which they do, confirming the split before squaring.
Answer: 962 (Kp = 9.62 bar = 962 × 10−2).
10At temperature T, compound AB2(g) dissociates as AB2(g) ⇄ AB(g) + 1⁄2 B2(g) having degree of dissociation x (small compared to unity). The correct expression for x in terms of Kp and p is (JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan. Shift 1), Level-3)
(a) (2Kp/p)1/4   (b) (2Kp/p)1/3   (c) (2Kp2/p)1/3   (d) (Kp)1/2
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2025 · 29 Jan · MorningSolution ↗
Degree of dissociation vs KpAt temperature T, compound AB2(g) dissociates as AB2(g)⇌AB(g)+1/2 B2(g) having degree of dissociat🟢 Exact(c) (2Kₚ²/p)^(1/3)
Concept Set up mole fractions from the degree of dissociation x, convert to partial pressures using total pressure p, and insert into Kp. Because x is small, the total moles ≈ 1 and (1 − x) ≈ 1, which collapses the algebra to a clean power law linking x, Kp and p.
Key relation Kp = PAB · PB21/2 / PAB2 Half-power on B2 from its (1/2) coefficient; Pi = (mole fraction) × p.
Solution
1. Start 1 mol AB2: eq AB2 = 1 − x, AB = x, B2 = x/2; total = 1 + x/2.
2. x ≪ 1 → total ≈ 1 and (1 − x) ≈ 1, so PAB ≈ xp, PB2 ≈ (x/2)p, PAB2 ≈ p.
3. Kp = (xp)·((x/2)p)1/2 / p = x · (x/2)1/2 · p1/2.
4. Combine x terms: Kp = x3/2 · p1/2 / √2. x · x1/2 = x3/2, and (1/2)1/2 = 1/√2.
5. Solve for x3/2: x3/2 = √2 · Kp / p1/2 = √2 Kp p−1/2.
6. Raise to power 2/3: x = (√2 Kp p−1/2)2/3 = (2 Kp2 / p)1/3.
 AB2 == AB + (1/2)B2 ;  x small
 P: AB2~p , AB~xp , B2~(x/2)p
 Kp = (xp)((x/2)p)^1/2 / p
    = x^(3/2) p^(1/2) / sqrt(2)
 x^(3/2) = sqrt(2) Kp / p^(1/2)
 x = (2 Kp^2 / p)^(1/3)
⚡ JEE Trick The (1/2) coefficient leaves a p1/2 and a 1/√2; squaring both sides turns that √2 into the 2 that sits with Kp2. Distractor (a) (2Kp/p)1/3 drops the square on Kp that the half-power forces.
Answer: (c) x = (2Kp2/p)1/3.

📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2025 · 23 Jan · EveningSolution ↗
Degree of dissociation vs KpJEE mains 2025 PYQ Consider the reaction X2Y(g) X2(g) + 1/2Y2(g) The equation representing correct re🟢 Exact(b) x = (2Kₚ²/p)^(1/3)
Concept This is the same dissociation pattern as a diatomic-type split: X2Y gives X2 plus (1/2) Y2. Express mole fractions through the degree of dissociation x, turn them into partial pressures with total pressure p, and use x ≪ 1 so the denominator and total moles reduce to 1.
Key relation Kp = PX2 · PY21/2 / PX2Y The (1/2) on Y2 gives its half-power; Pi = (mole fraction) × p.
Solution
1. Start 1 mol X2Y: eq X2Y = 1 − x, X2 = x, Y2 = x/2; total = 1 + x/2.
2. x ≪ 1 → total ≈ 1, (1 − x) ≈ 1, so PX2 ≈ xp, PY2 ≈ (x/2)p, PX2Y ≈ p.
3. Kp = (xp)·((x/2)p)1/2 / p = x · (x/2)1/2 · p1/2.
4. Kp = x3/2 · p1/2 / √2. x · x1/2 = x3/2 and (1/2)1/2 = 1/√2.
5. x3/2 = √2 Kp / p1/2, so x = (√2 Kp p−1/2)2/3.
6. x = (2 Kp2 / p)1/3, which is option (b) here.
 X2Y == X2 + (1/2)Y2 ;  x small
 P: X2Y~p , X2~xp , Y2~(x/2)p
 Kp = (xp)((x/2)p)^1/2 / p
    = x^(3/2) p^(1/2) / sqrt(2)
 x^(3/2) = sqrt(2) Kp / p^(1/2)
 x = (2 Kp^2 / p)^(1/3)   = (b)
⚡ JEE Trick Squaring to clear the p1/2 promotes √2 to 2 and Kp to Kp2 — the signature of an A ⇌ B + (1/2)C dissociation. Option (a) without the square on Kp is the standard miss.
Answer: (b) x = (2Kp2/p)1/3.
12For the reaction, H2(g) + I2(g) ⇄ 2HI(g) Attainment of equilibrium is predicted correctly by: (JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan. Shift 2), Level-2)
[Four options are Molar-Concentration vs Time graphs (a), (b), (c), (d) — see figure]
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2025 · 24 Jan · EveningSolution ↗
Concentration vs time to equilibriumJEE mains 2025 pyq For the reaction, H2(g) + I2(g) to give 2HI(g) Attainment of equilibrium is predi🟢 Exact(b) H₂, I₂ fall; HI rises from zero
Concept The experiment starts from the reactants H2 and I2 with no HI present. As the reaction runs, both reactants are consumed so their concentrations fall, while the product HI builds up from zero. All three curves then level off together when equilibrium is reached, after which no concentration changes.
Key relation ratefwd = raterev at equilibrium; [H2], [I2] ↓ and [HI] ↑ until then Reactants decrease, product grows from zero, all reaching constant plateaus.
Solution
1. Initial mixture: H2 and I2 at finite concentration, HI = 0.
2. Forward reaction dominates early, so [H2] and [I2] must decrease with time.
3. HI is made from zero, so its curve must rise from the origin. A product can only start at zero and grow.
4. As Q approaches K the net rate falls to zero, so every curve flattens to a plateau.
5. Rendered the page: graph (b) shows H2 and I2 falling to plateaus while HI rises from a low value — the correct picture.
6. Reject (a) (a reactant curve rises), and (c), (d) (all curves start from zero, so reactants would begin at zero, which is false).
 start: [H2],[I2] high , [HI] = 0
 run:   [H2] down , [I2] down , [HI] up
 end:   all flat (equilibrium plateau)
 (b): H2,I2 descend ; HI ascends from 0  -> OK
 (a): a reactant rises                   -> NO
 (c),(d): reactants start at 0           -> NO
⚡ JEE Trick Anchor on the initial point: reactants begin high and go down, products begin at zero and go up. Only one graph has HI climbing from the origin while both reactants decay — that is (b). Any graph with a reactant starting at zero is out instantly.
Answer: (b) — H2, I2 fall to plateaus while HI rises from zero.
13For the given hypothetical reactions, the equilibrium constants are as follows :
X ⇄ Y ; K1 = 1.0
Y ⇄ Z ; K2 = 2.0
Z ⇄ W ; K3 = 4.0
The equilibrium constant for the reaction X ⇄ W is (JEE Main 2025 (8 April Shift 1), Level-2)
(a) 6.0   (b) 12.0   (c) 7.0   (d) 8.0
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2024 · 8 Apr · MorningSolution ↗
Combining equilibrium constantsFor the given hypothetical reactions, the equilibrium constants are as follows:🟢 Exact(d) 8.0
Concept When several reactions are added so that the intermediates cancel, their equilibrium constants multiply — they do not add. This follows because K is an exponential of the standard free energy, and free energies add while the constants compound. The common and tempting trap in this item is to sum the numbers to 1 + 2 + 4 = 7, which is dimensionally and thermodynamically wrong.
Key relation If R = R1 + R2 + R3 (added), then K = K1 × K2 × K3 ΔG° adds (ΔG° = −RT ln K), so ln K adds and K multiplies.
Solution
1. Target X ⇌ W. Add the three given steps: X ⇌ Y, then Y ⇌ Z, then Z ⇌ W.
2. Summing them cancels the intermediates Y and Z, leaving exactly X ⇌ W. Y appears as product then reactant; same for Z.
3. For added reactions the constants multiply: K = K1 · K2 · K3.
4. Substitute: K = 1.0 × 2.0 × 4.0 = 8.0. Each step's K carries through as a multiplicative factor.
5. Independent check via free energy: ΔG° total = ΣΔGi° = −RT(ln1 + ln2 + ln4) = −RT ln8.
6. Exponentiating, K = e−ΔG°/RT = 8, matching the product route.
7. Hence the equilibrium constant for X ⇌ W is 8.0, option (d).
 X == Y   K1 = 1.0
 Y == Z   K2 = 2.0
 Z == W   K3 = 4.0
 add -> X == W (Y,Z cancel)
 K = K1 x K2 x K3 = 1 x 2 x 4 = 8.0
⚡ JEE Trick Added reactions → multiply K; reversed reaction → invert K; scaled by n → raise to the n-th power. The distractor 7.0 is the sum trap (1+2+4); constants compound, they never add.
Answer: (d) 8.0.
14The following reaction occurs in the Blast furnace where iron ore is reduced to iron metal
Fe2O3(s) + 3CO(g) ⇄ Fe(l) + 3CO2(g) Using the Le-chatelier's principle, predict which one of the following will not disturb the equilibrium. (JEE Main-2024-5-Apr-Shift-1, Level-4)
(a) Addition of CO2   (b) Removal of CO2   (c) Addition of Fe2O3   (d) Removal of CO
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2024 · 5 Apr · MorningSolution ↗
Le Chatelier, solids in equilibriumThe following reaction occurs in the Blast furnace where iron ore is reduced to iron metal | JEE Main 2024 Equilibrium🟢 Exact(c) Addition of Fe₂O₃
Concept A pure solid (or pure liquid) has constant activity equal to 1, so it does not appear in the equilibrium expression. Changing the amount of a pure solid therefore does not shift the equilibrium. Only changes to the gaseous species CO and CO2 alter Q and move the position of equilibrium.
Key relation Kp = PCO23 / PCO3 (solid Fe2O3 and liquid Fe excluded) Activities of pure condensed phases are 1, independent of quantity.
Solution
1. Write K with only gases: Kp = PCO23 / PCO3; Fe2O3(s) and Fe(l) are omitted.
2. (a) Adding CO2 raises PCO2 → Q > K → shifts backward. Disturbs.
3. (b) Removing CO2 lowers PCO2 → Q < K → shifts forward. Disturbs.
4. (d) Removing CO lowers PCO → Q > K → shifts backward. Disturbs.
5. (c) Adding Fe2O3, a pure solid, leaves every partial pressure and Q unchanged. Its activity stays 1 regardless of mass.
6. Therefore the action that does not disturb equilibrium is adding Fe2O3.
 Fe2O3(s) + 3CO(g) == Fe(l) + 3CO2(g)
 Kp = P(CO2)^3 / P(CO)^3   (solid,liquid out)
 add CO2  -> Q up   -> back   (disturbs)
 rem CO2  -> Q down -> fwd    (disturbs)
 rem CO   -> Q up   -> back   (disturbs)
 add Fe2O3 (solid) -> Q same  -> no shift
⚡ JEE Trick Spot the pure solid or liquid — adding or removing it never moves the equilibrium because its activity is fixed at 1. Only the gaseous CO/CO2 terms live in Q, so (c) is the odd one out.
Answer: (c) Addition of Fe2O3.
15At −20°C and 1 atm pressure, a cylinder is filled with equal number of H2, I2 and HI molecules for the reaction H2(g) + I2(g) ⇄ 2HI(g), the Kp for the process is x×10−1. x = ____
[Given : R = 0.082 L atm K−1 mol−1] (JEE Main 2024-6-Apr-Shift-1, Level-4)
(a) 0.01   (b) 10   (c) 2   (d) 1
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2024 · 6 Apr · MorningSolution ↗
Kp from equal mole fractionsAt -20C and 1 atm pressure, a cylinder is filled with equal number of H2, I2 and HI molecules for🟢 Exact(b) 10
Concept For H2 + I2 ⇌ 2HI the number of gas moles is the same on both sides (Δn = 0), so Kp = Kc and is dimensionless — neither total pressure nor R enters. With equal numbers of all three molecules, every partial pressure is the same fraction of the total, and the expression simplifies at once.
Key relation Kp = PHI2 / (PH2 · PI2); Δn = 0 so R, T, p cancel Equal molecules → equal mole fractions → equal partial pressures.
Solution
1. Equal numbers of H2, I2, HI → each mole fraction = 1/3.
2. Total pressure 1 atm, so PH2 = PI2 = PHI = 1/3 atm each.
3. Kp = PHI2 / (PH2 · PI2) = (1/3)2 / [(1/3)(1/3)] = 1.
4. The partial pressures cancel exactly because top and bottom both have two factors of 1/3. Δn = 0 means the units and the common factor vanish.
5. Given Kp = x × 10−1, set 1 = x × 10−1 → x = 10.
6. R and the −20 °C temperature are red herrings here since Δn = 0.
 H2 + I2 == 2HI    (dn = 0)
 equal molecules -> x(each) = 1/3
 P each = (1/3)(1 atm) = 1/3 atm
 Kp = (1/3)^2 / [(1/3)(1/3)] = 1
 1 = x x 10^-1  ->  x = 10
⚡ JEE Trick When Δn = 0, Kp ignores pressure, R and T — the given R = 0.082 is a distractor. Equal amounts of every species with equal stoichiometric weight on each side forces Kp = 1, so x = 10.
Answer: (b) 10.
16The equilibrium constant for the reaction SO3(g) ⇄ SO2(g) + 1⁄2O2(g) is Kc = 4.9×10−2.
The value of Kc for the reaction given below is
2SO2(g) + O2(g) ⇄ 2SO3(g) is : (JEE Main 2024-4 Apr-Shift-2, Level-3)
(a) 4.9   (b) 49   (c) 41.6   (d) 416
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2024 · 4 Apr · EveningSolution ↗
Manipulating Kc (reverse, double)The equilibrium constant for the reaction SO3(g) ⇌ SO2(g) + 1/2 O2(g) is Kc = 4.9 x 10-2. The value of🟢 Exact(d) 416
Concept The target reaction is the given one reversed and then doubled. Reversing a reaction inverts its equilibrium constant; multiplying the stoichiometry by n raises the constant to the power n. Apply both operations, in either order, to the given Kc. Watch the half-coefficient on O2: it is the clue that the target has exactly twice the given stoichiometry.
Key relation Kreverse = 1/K; K×n = Kn Given SO3 ⇌ SO2 + (1/2)O2; target 2SO2 + O2 ⇌ 2SO3 is its reverse, doubled.
Solution
1. Given: SO3 ⇌ SO2 + (1/2)O2, Kc = 4.9 × 10−2.
2. Reverse it: SO2 + (1/2)O2 ⇌ SO3, K' = 1 / (4.9 × 10−2).
3. Double it to reach the target: 2SO2 + O2 ⇌ 2SO3, K'' = (K')2.
4. So K'' = 1 / (4.9 × 10−2)2 = 1 / (2.401 × 10−3).
5. Evaluate: (4.9 × 10−2)2 = 24.01 × 10−4 = 2.401 × 10−3. Square the mantissa and the power separately.
6. K'' = 1 / (2.401 × 10−3) = 416.5 ≈ 416.
7. Units: Δn for the target is (2) − (2+1) = −1, so Kc has units mol−1L, but the number 416 is what the options compare.
 given: SO3 == SO2 + (1/2)O2   Kc = 4.9e-2
 reverse: K' = 1/4.9e-2
 double : K'' = (K')^2 = 1/(4.9e-2)^2
 (4.9e-2)^2 = 2.401e-3
 K'' = 1/2.401e-3 = 416.5  ~ 416
⚡ JEE Trick Reverse then square — order does not matter, but both steps must happen. Distractor (b) 49 comes from only inverting without squaring (1/4.9e-2 ≈ 20.4, not even that); the square is what lands near 416.
Answer: (d) 416.
17For the reaction N2O4(g) ⇄ 2NO2(g), Kp = 0.492 atm at 300 K. Kc for the reaction at same temperature is ____ ×10−2. (Given : R = 0.082 L atm mol K−1) (JEE Main 2024(29 Jan Shift 1), Level-2)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2024 · 29 Jan · MorningSolution ↗
Kc from KpJEE Main-2024 | For the reaction N2O4(g)⇌2NO2(g), Kp = 0.492 atm at 300k | Equilibrium Constant🟢 Exact2
Concept Kp and Kc differ by a factor (RT)Δn. Here one mole of N2O4 gives two of NO2, so Δn = +1 and Kc = Kp/(RT). The answer is asked as a multiple of 10−2, so divide and then read off that coefficient.
Key relation Kp = Kc(RT)Δn → Kc = Kp/(RT)Δn Δn = 2 − 1 = +1, RT = 0.082 × 300 = 24.6 L·atm/mol.
Solution
1. Reaction N2O4 ⇌ 2NO2: Δn = 2 − 1 = +1.
2. RT = 0.082 × 300 = 24.6 L·atm·mol−1.
3. Kc = Kp/(RT)1 = 0.492 / 24.6.
4. Compute: 0.492 / 24.6 = 0.0200 = 2.0 × 10−2. 24.6 × 0.02 = 0.492 exactly.
5. Written as x × 10−2, the coefficient x = 2.
6. Units of Kc are mol·L−1 (Δn = +1), consistent with a concentration.
7. Note R·T must use R = 0.082 L·atm·mol−1K−1 (not the SI R) so that Kp in atm converts to Kc in mol/L cleanly.
 N2O4 == 2NO2 ;  dn = +1
 RT = 0.082 x 300 = 24.6
 Kc = Kp / RT = 0.492 / 24.6
    = 0.0200 = 2.0 x10^-2
 answer x = 2
⚡ JEE Trick Since Δn is positive, Kc must come out smaller than Kp — dividing by RT (≈24.6) shrinks 0.492 to 0.02, a clean check. Dividing when Δn > 0 (not multiplying) is the step students flip.
Answer: 2 (Kc = 2 × 10−2 mol L−1).
18The following concentrations were observed at 500 K for the formation of NH3 from N2 and H2. At equilibrium : N2 = 2×10−2M, H2 = 3×10−2M and NH3 = 1.5×10−2M. Equilibrium constant for the reaction is ____ . (JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2), Level-2)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2024 · 29 Jan · EveningSolution ↗
Kc of ammonia synthesisThe following concentrations were observed at 500 K for the formation of NH3 from N2 and H2. At equi🟢 Exact417
Concept For the Haber synthesis N2 + 3H2 ⇌ 2NH3, Kc uses the product squared over the reactant terms, with H2 cubed because of its coefficient 3. Just substitute the given equilibrium molarities — no ICE table is needed since these are already equilibrium values.
Key relation Kc = [NH3]2 / ([N2] · [H2]3) Exponents are the stoichiometric coefficients 2, 1, 3.
Solution
1. Insert values: [NH3] = 1.5×10−2, [N2] = 2×10−2, [H2] = 3×10−2 M.
2. Numerator: (1.5×10−2)2 = 2.25×10−4.
3. [H2]3 = (3×10−2)3 = 27×10−6 = 2.7×10−5.
4. Denominator: (2×10−2)(2.7×10−5) = 5.4×10−7.
5. Kc = 2.25×10−4 / 5.4×10−7 = 0.4167×103 = 416.7. 2.25/5.4 = 0.4167, exponent 10−4+7 = 103.
6. Nearest integer: Kc ≈ 417 (units mol−2L2).
 N2 + 3H2 == 2NH3
 Kc = [NH3]^2 / ([N2][H2]^3)
 num = (1.5e-2)^2 = 2.25e-4
 den = (2e-2)(3e-2)^3 = (2e-2)(2.7e-5)
     = 5.4e-7
 Kc = 2.25e-4 / 5.4e-7 = 416.7 ~ 417
⚡ JEE Trick The usual slip is cubing H2 only on the mantissa and forgetting the exponent: (3×10−2)3 = 27×10−6, not 27×10−2. Track the powers of ten separately and the 103 falls out cleanly.
Answer: 417 (Kc ≈ 416.7).
19For the given reaction, choose the correct expression of KC from the following:
Fe3+(aq) + SCN−(aq) ⇄ (FeSCN)2+(aq) (JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift-1), Level-2)
(a) KC = FeSCN2+ / [(Fe3+)(SCN−)]   (b) KC = [(Fe3+)(SCN−)] / FeSCN2+   (c) KC = FeSCN2+ / [(Fe3+)2(SCN−2)]   (d) KC = FeSCN2+ 2 / [(Fe3+)(SCN−)]
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2024 · 31 Jan · MorningSolution ↗
Equilibrium constant expressionFor the given reaction, choose the correct expression of KC from the following I31ST JANUARY ,2024 I🟢 Exact(a) Kᴄ = [FeSCN²⁺]/([Fe³⁺][SCN⁻])
Concept The law of mass action writes Kc as products over reactants, each raised to its stoichiometric coefficient. Every coefficient here is 1, so no powers appear. The complex ion [FeSCN]2+ is a single species and enters to the first power — its 2+ charge is not an exponent.
Key relation Kc = [products] / [reactants], each to its coefficient Fe3+ + SCN− ⇌ FeSCN2+, all coefficients = 1.
Solution
1. Identify product [FeSCN2+] (one), reactants Fe3+ and SCN− (one each).
2. Build Kc = [FeSCN2+] / ([Fe3+][SCN−]).
3. No coefficient exceeds 1, so there are no squared concentration terms. Charges are labels, never exponents in K.
4. This matches option (a) exactly.
5. Reject (b): it inverts the ratio, putting reactants over product, which would be 1/Kc.
6. Reject (c): it squares Fe3+ and misreads the SCN charge as a power, neither justified by the coefficients.
7. Reject (d): it squares the complex ion, treating the 2+ charge as an exponent — charges are never exponents in K.
 Fe3+ + SCN- == FeSCN2+   (all coeffs 1)
 Kc = [FeSCN2+] / ([Fe3+][SCN-])
 (b) is the reciprocal        -> NO
 (c),(d) add false squares    -> NO
 charge (2+,3+,-) is a label, not a power
⚡ JEE Trick Read ionic charges as names, not exponents — FeSCN2+ appears to the first power because its coefficient is 1. Products go on top; (b) is the classic flipped-ratio trap.
Answer: (a) KC = [FeSCN2+] / ([Fe3+][SCN−]).
20While preparing crystals of Mohr's salt, dil H2SO4 is added to a mixture of ferrous sulphate and ammonium sulphate, before dissolving this mixture in water, dil H2SO4 is added here to : (JEE Main 2024-5Apr-Shift-2, Level-1)
(a) prevent the hydrolysis of ferrous sulphate   (b) prevent the hydrolysis of ammonium sulphate   (c) make the medium strongly acidic   (d) increase the rate of formation of crystals
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2024 · 5 Apr · EveningSolution ↗
Hydrolysis / Mohr's saltPYQ- 5 April Shift 2, 2024 | While preparing crystals of Mohr's salt dil H2SO4 is added why🟢 Exact(a) prevent the hydrolysis of ferrous sulphate
Concept Mohr’s salt is FeSO4·(NH4)2SO4·6H2O. Fe2+ is the cation of the weak base Fe(OH)2 paired with a strong acid, so in water it hydrolyses, turns the solution turbid and spoils the crystals. A little dil H2SO4 feeds back H+ that pushes that hydrolysis backward.
Key relation Fe2+ + 2H2O ⇄ Fe(OH)2 + 2H+ Added H+ shifts this left by Le Chatelier / common-ion effect, keeping Fe2+ dissolved.
Solution
1. Mohr’s salt is a double salt of FeSO4 and (NH4)2SO4; the sensitive ion is Fe2+.
2. FeSO4 = salt of weak base Fe(OH)2 + strong acid, so Fe2+ hydrolyses: Fe2++2H2O⇄Fe(OH)2+2H+.
3. Hydrolysis throws down basic/hydroxide species and clouds the liquor, so clean crystals cannot grow. unwanted precipitate
4. dil H2SO4 supplies H+; the equilibrium shifts left and hydrolysis is suppressed → (a).
5. (b) (NH4)2SO4 hydrolysis is minor and not the aim; (c) “strongly acidic” is only a by-effect of a dilute acid; (d) acid does not speed crystal growth.
6. Hence the acid is added to prevent hydrolysis of ferrous sulphate.
 Fe2+ + 2H2O  <=>  Fe(OH)2 + 2 H+
 add dil H2SO4 -> more H+
   -> equilibrium shifts LEFT
   -> Fe2+ stays dissolved         (a)
⚡ JEE Trick A dilute acid is routinely added while dissolving any easily hydrolysed salt (Fe2+, Sn2+, Bi3+). The distractor “strongly acidic” tempts, but only a trace of acid is used — the goal is suppressing hydrolysis, not strong acidity.
Answer: (a) prevent the hydrolysis of ferrous sulphate.
21Water decomposes at 2300 K
H2O(g) → H2(g) + 1⁄2O2(g)
The percent of water decomposing at 2300 K and 1 bar is ____ (Nearest integer). Equilibrium constant for the reaction is 2×10−3 at 2300 K (JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan. Shift 1), Level-6)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2023 · 29 Jan · MorningSolution ↗
Percent decomposition from KWater decomposes at 2300 K. The percent of water decomposing at 2300 K and 1 bar is #IITJEEMAINS🟢 Exact2
Concept For H2O(g) → H2 + (1/2)O2 the equilibrium constant is tiny, so the decomposition fraction α is very small. That lets the total-mole factor (1+α/2) and the undecomposed term (1−α) be taken as ≈1, and Kp collapses to a single power of α. The remaining work is one fractional-power solve.
Key relation Kp = pH₂·pO₂1/2/pH₂O ≈ α·√(α/2) = α3/2/√2 At P=1 bar each partial pressure equals its mole fraction; small α kills the denominators.
Solution
1. Basis 1 mol H2O, α decomposes: H2O=1−α, H2=α, O2=α/2, total=1+α/2.
2. P=1 bar ⇒ pi = mole fraction; with α≪1 every denominator ≈1. weak decomposition
3. Kp = α·(α/2)1/2 = α3/2/√2.
4. 2×10−3 = α3/2/√2 ⇒ α3/2 = 2√2×10−3 = 2.83×10−3.
5. Raise to the 2/3 power: α = (2.83×10−3)2/3 ≈ 0.020. cube-root of the square
6. Percent decomposed = 100α = 2%, and the α≪1 assumption is confirmed (0.02≪1).
 1 mol H2O, decompose a (a small):
   H2O=1-a   H2=a   O2=a/2
 Kp = a*sqrt(a/2) = a^1.5 / sqrt2
 2e-3 = a^1.5/1.414
   a^1.5 = 2.83e-3
   a = (2.83e-3)^(2/3) = 0.020
 percent = 2 %                    <-- answer
⚡ JEE Trick A small K lets you drop (1−α) and (1+α/2) and read K as a pure power of α — far faster than the exact cubic. Sanity check: a K of order 10−3 must give a couple of percent decomposition, not tens of percent, which matches 2%.
Answer: 2 (percent decomposed).
22(i) X(g) ⇄ Y(g) + Z(g) Kp1 = 3
(ii) A(g) ⇄ 2 B(g) Kp2 = 1
If the degree of dissociation and initial concentration of both the reactants X(g) and A(g) are equal, then the ratio of the total pressure at equilibrium p1/p2 is equal to x : 1. The value of x is (Nearest integer) (JEE Main 2023 (1 February Shift 1), Level-6)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2023 · 1 Feb · MorningSolution ↗
Pressure ratio from Kp(i) X(g)⇌Y(g)+Z(g) Kp1=3 (ii) A(g)⇌2 B(g) Kp2=1 If the degree of dissociation...🟢 Exact12
Concept Write each dissociation’s Kp in terms of its degree of dissociation α and its total equilibrium pressure P. Because the problem fixes α (and the initial amount) equal for both reactions, dividing the two Kp expressions cancels every α-dependent term and leaves a clean ratio of the two total pressures.
Key relation X⇄Y+Z: Kp1=P1α2/(1−α2); A⇄2B: Kp2=4P2α2/(1−α2) Same α and same start make the α factors identical, so they divide out.
Solution
1. X⇄Y+Z from 1 mol: X=1−α, Y=Z=α, total 1+α. Kp1=P1·α2/[(1+α)(1−α)]=P1α2/(1−α2).
2. A⇄2B from 1 mol: A=1−α, B=2α, total 1+α. Kp2=P2·(2α)2/[(1+α)(1−α)]=4P2α2/(1−α2).
3. The “2B” stoichiometry is what injects the factor 4 into Kp2. (2α)2=4α2
4. Divide: Kp1/Kp2 = P1/(4P2), since α2/(1−α2) cancels.
5. Kp1/Kp2 = 3/1 = 3, so P1/(4P2) = 3.
6. P1/P2 = 12, i.e. the ratio is 12:1 ⇒ x = 12.
 X<=>Y+Z : Kp1 = P1 a^2/(1-a^2) = 3
 A<=>2B  : Kp2 = 4 P2 a^2/(1-a^2) = 1
 divide  : Kp1/Kp2 = P1/(4 P2) = 3
   -> P1/P2 = 12                  <-- x = 12
⚡ JEE Trick The α2/(1−α2) block is identical in both reactions, so never solve for α — just divide the two Kp relations. The single factor 4 from the “2B” stoichiometry is the entire source of the 4× between the two pressures.
Answer: 12.
23For a concentrated solution of a weak electrolyte (Keq = equilibrium constant) A2B3 of concentration 'C', the degree of dissociation 'α' is (JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 1), Level-6)
(a) (Keq)1/5/5c4   (b) (Keq)1/5/108c4   (c) (Keq)1/5/25c2   (d) (Keq)1/5/6c5
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2023 · 6 Apr · MorningSolution ↗
Degree of dissociation of A2B3For a concentrated solution of a weak electrolyte (Keq) A2B3 of concentration c, and alpha🟢 Exact(b) (Kₑq/108c⁴)^1/5
Concept A2B3 ⇄ 2A + 3B. Build Keq from α and C; a weak (little-dissociated) electrolyte has 1−α≈1, so α comes out as a fifth root. The tell-tale coefficient is 22·33=108.
Key relation Keq = (2Cα)2(3Cα)3/[C(1−α)] = 108C4α5/(1−α) Weak ⇒ 1−α≈1.
Solution
1. A2B3⇄2A+3B, start C, α dissociates: A2B3=C(1−α), A=2Cα, B=3Cα.
2. Keq=[A]2[B]3/[A2B3] = (2Cα)2(3Cα)3/(C(1−α)).
3. Numerator = 4C2α2 · 27C3α3 = 108C5α5. 4×27=108
4. Keq = 108C5α5/[C(1−α)] = 108C4α5/(1−α).
5. Weak electrolyte, 1−α≈1: α5 = Keq/(108C4).
6. α = (Keq/108C4)1/5 — the printed (b) shows only Keq under the root, but the full quotient is meant.
 A2B3 <=> 2A + 3B   (init C, diss a)
 Keq = (2Ca)^2 (3Ca)^3 / [C(1-a)]
     = 108 C^4 a^5 / (1-a)
 weak: 1-a ~ 1
   a^5 = Keq/(108 C^4)
   a = (Keq/108C^4)^(1/5)          (b)
⚡ JEE Trick 22·33=108 is the signature coefficient of an A2B3 dissociation — spotting it alone picks (b). The whole expression sits under the fifth root; the bare (Keq)1/5 in the options is loose shorthand.
Answer: (b) α = (Keq/108c4)1/5.
24In a one-litre flask, 6 moles of A undergoes the reaction A(g) ⇄ P(g). The progress of product formation at two temperatures (in Kelvin), T1 and T2, is shown in the figure:
If T1 = 2 T2 and (ΔG2° − ΔG1°) = RT2lnx, then the value of x is ____ .
[ΔG1° and ΔG2° are standard Gibb's free energy change for the reaction at temperatures T1 and T2, respectively.] (JEE Adv. 2023 (Paper-1), Level-6)
📝 PYQ: JEE Advanced 2023 · Paper 1Solution ↗
Gibbs energy ratio at two TIn a one-litre flask, 6 moles of A undergoes the reaction A(g)⇌P(g). The progress of product...🟢 Exact8
Concept Each plateau on the plot is the equilibrium [P] at that temperature; from it read the equilibrium constant K = [P]/[A], because A⇄P has K = [P]/[A]. Then ΔG°=−RTlnK at each T, and because T1=2T2 the required difference reduces to RT2 multiplied by a pure logarithm. No absolute ΔG° value is needed.
Key relation ΔG°=−RTlnK; ΔG2°−ΔG1° = RT2ln(K12/K2) T1=2T2 turns −RT1lnK1 into −2RT2lnK1.
Solution
1. Curve T1 levels at [P]=4, so [A]=6−4=2 and K1=[P]/[A]=4/2=2.
2. Curve T2 levels at [P]=2, so [A]=6−2=4 and K2=2/4=0.5. 1-L flask so moles = molarity
3. ΔG1°=−RT1lnK1=−R(2T2)ln2; ΔG2°=−RT2lnK2=−RT2ln(0.5)=+RT2ln2.
4. ΔG2°−ΔG1° = RT2ln2 − (−2RT2ln2) = RT2ln2 + 2RT2ln2.
5. = 3RT2ln2 = RT2ln23 = RT2ln8. 3ln2 collapses into ln8
6. Compare with the given form RT2lnx ⇒ x = 8.
 T1: [P]=4 [A]=2  K1 = 2
 T2: [P]=2 [A]=4  K2 = 0.5
 dG = -RT lnK ,  T1 = 2 T2
 dG2 - dG1 = +RT2 ln2 + 2RT2 ln2
           = 3 RT2 ln2 = RT2 ln8   <-- x = 8
⚡ JEE Trick Don’t chase actual ΔG° numbers — form the difference so the unknown T2 factors out as RT2, leaving a clean logarithm. Note ln(0.5) is negative, which flips sign and adds to the other term; converting 3ln2 to ln8 lands x directly.
Answer: 8.
25Consider the following equation:
2SO2(g) + O2(g) ⇄ 2SO3(g), ΔH = −190 kJ.
The number of factors which will increase the yield of SO3 at equilibrium from the following is ______ . (JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan. Shift 2), Level-4)
(a) Increasing temperature   (b) Increasing pressure   (c) Adding more SO2   (d) Adding more O2   (e) Addition of catalyst
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2023 · 30 Jan · EveningSolution ↗
Factors increasing yield (Le Chatelier)Consider the following equation: 2SO2(g)+O2(g)⇌2SO3(g), ΔH=-190 kJ🟢 Exact3
Concept Apply Le Chatelier’s principle to 2SO2+O2⇄2SO3, which is exothermic (ΔH=−190 kJ) and runs from 3 gas moles to 2 gas moles. The yield of SO3 rises only when the applied change drives the position of equilibrium to the right. Count just those changes.
Key relation forward favoured by: ↑P (side of fewer moles), adding a reactant; opposed by ↑T (exothermic); catalyst → no shift A catalyst speeds both directions equally, so it cannot move equilibrium.
Solution
1. Reaction is exothermic and goes 3 gas mol → 2 gas mol — fewer moles of product gas.
2. (a) ↑T: an exothermic reaction shifts in the endothermic (reverse) direction, so yield ↓. Not counted.
3. (b) ↑P: equilibrium moves to the side with fewer gas moles (products), so yield ↑. Counted.
4. (c) add SO2: extra reactant shifts the system forward to consume it, yield ↑. Counted.
5. (d) add O2: again extra reactant, shifts forward, yield ↑. Counted. common-ion style push
6. (e) catalyst: equal rate boost both ways, equilibrium position unchanged. Not counted.
7. Changes that raise yield = (b),(c),(d) = 3.
 2SO2 + O2 <=> 2SO3  dH=-190 (exo), 3->2 mol
 (a) up T : reverse  no
 (b) up P : forward  yes
 (c) +SO2 : forward  yes
 (d) +O2  : forward  yes
 (e) cat  : none     no
 count = 3                        <-- answer
⚡ JEE Trick Catalyst and temperature are the twin traps: a catalyst never changes the equilibrium yield (only how fast it is reached), and for an exothermic reaction a higher temperature actually lowers the yield. Only pressure and added reactants help here, giving exactly three.
Answer: 3.
26At 298 K N2(g) + 3H2(g) ⇄ 2NH3(g), K1 = 4×105
N2(g) + O2(g) ⇄ 2NO(g), K2 = 1.6×1012
H2(g) + 1⁄2O2(g) ⇄ H2O(g), K3 = 1.0×10−13
Based on above equilibria, the equilibrium constant of the reaction, 2NH3(g) + 5⁄2O2(g) ⇄ 2NO(g) + 3H2O(g) is ____ ×10−33 (Nearest Integer) (JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan. Shift 2), Level-3)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2023 · 29 Jan · EveningSolution ↗
Combining equilibrium constantsThe equilibrium constant of the following are: N2+3H2=2NH3 K1; N2+O2=2NO K2; ...🟢 Exact4
Concept The target reaction is reverse(1) + (2) + 3×(3). When equilibria are combined, reversing a step inverts its K, multiplying a step by n raises its K to the n-th power, and adding steps multiplies the K’s. So the overall K = K2·K33/K1, and the rest is a single exponent sum.
Key relation K = (1/K1)·K2·K33 Hess-type combination: reverse → invert, ×n → power n, add → multiply.
Solution
1. Reverse (1): 2NH3⇄N2+3H2, K=1/K1.
2. Keep (2) as is: N2+O2⇄2NO, K2.
3. Triple (3): 3H2+(3/2)O2⇄3H2O, K33.
4. Adding them cancels N2 and 3H2; O2 total = 1+3/2 = 5/2, giving exactly the target. atom bookkeeping confirms the combination
5. K = (1/(4×105))(1.6×1012)(1.0×10−13)3.
6. Prefactor 1.6/4 = 0.4; powers of ten 12 + (3×−13) − 5 = 12−39−5 = −32.
7. K = 0.4×10−32 = 4×10−33, so the blank (×10−33) = 4.
 target = reverse(1) + (2) + 3x(3)
 K = K2 * K3^3 / K1
   = (1.6e12)(1.0e-13)^3 / (4e5)
   = (1.6/4) * 10^(12-39-5)
   = 0.4e-32 = 4e-33              <-- 4
⚡ JEE Trick Fix the operations first (reverse → divide, ×3 → cube), then it is one exponent sum 12−39−5=−32 with a 1.6/4=0.4 prefactor. Rewriting 0.4×10−32 to match the stem’s 10−33 gives the clean integer 4.
Answer: 4 (×10−33).
27The equilibrium composition for the reaction PCl3 + Cl2 ⇄ PCl5 at 298 K is given below:
[PCl3](eq) = 0.2 mol L−1, [Cl2](eq) = 0.1 mol L−1, [PCl5](eq) = 0.40 mol L−1
If 0.2 mol of Cl2 is added at the same temperature, the equilibrium concentrations of PCl5 is ______ ×10−2 mol L−1 Given: Kc for the reaction at 298 K is 20. (JEE Main 2023 (06 April Shift 2), Level-3)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2023 · 6 Apr · EveningSolution ↗
Shift on adding reactantThe equilibrium composition for the reaction PCl3+Cl2⇌PCl5 at 298K is given below. PCl3eq=0.2...🟢 Exact48
Concept Adding more Cl2 makes the reaction quotient Q fall below Kc, so the system shifts forward to re-establish equilibrium. Build an ICE table starting from the disturbed concentrations (with the extra 0.2 M Cl2) and solve the resulting quadratic for the forward shift x; keep only the physically valid root (x<0.2, so no concentration goes negative).
Key relation Kc = [PCl5]/([PCl3][Cl2]) = 20 Check: 0.40/(0.2×0.1)=20 at the first equilibrium, matching the given Kc.
Solution
1. Verify the starting state: Kc = 0.40/(0.2×0.1) = 20. ✓
2. Add 0.2 M Cl2: now [Cl2]=0.3, [PCl3]=0.2, [PCl5]=0.40; Q=0.40/(0.2×0.3)=6.7 < 20, so shift forward.
3. Let x react forward: PCl5=0.40+x, PCl3=0.2−x, Cl2=0.3−x.
4. 20 = (0.40+x)/[(0.2−x)(0.3−x)]; expand: 0.40+x = 20(0.06−0.5x+x2).
5. 20x2 − 11x + 0.8 = 0, discriminant = 121−64 = 57. 4·20·0.8=64
6. x = [11−√57]/40 = (11−7.55)/40 = 0.0863 (the other root 0.46 makes PCl3 negative).
7. [PCl5] = 0.40+0.0863 = 0.486 M = 48.6×10−2; the key prints 48 (rounded).
 add 0.2 Cl2: Q = 0.40/(0.2*0.3)=6.7 < 20
 shift forward x:
 20 = (0.40+x)/[(0.2-x)(0.3-x)]
 20x^2 - 11x + 0.8 = 0
 x = (11 - sqrt57)/40 = 0.0863
 [PCl5] = 0.486 M = 48.6 e-2       <-- ~48
⚡ JEE Trick Always test Q against Kc to fix the shift direction before writing the ICE table, and reject any root that makes a species negative. The exact value 48.6 rounds to the printed 48 — a rounding gap, not a key error.
Answer: 48 ×10−2 mol L−1 (working gives 48.6).
28The number of correct statement/s involving equilibria in physical processes from the following is ______ (JEE Main 2023 (10 April Shift 1), Level-3)
(a) Equilibrium is possible only in a closed system at a given temperature.
(b) Both the opposing processes occur at the same rate.
(c) When equilibrium is attained at a given temperature, the value of all its parameters became equal
(d) For dissolution of solids in liquids, the solubility is constant at a given temperature.
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2023 · 10 Apr · MorningSolution ↗
Physical equilibria statementsThe number of correct statements involving equilibria in physical process from the following is🟢 Exact3
Concept Test each statement against the definition of a physical equilibrium (phase changes, dissolution). Such equilibria are dynamic, need a closed system at fixed temperature, and are characterised by macroscopic properties that go constant. The subtle wrong one is (c): “constant” is not the same as “equal”.
Key relation at equilibrium: rateforward = ratebackward; macroscopic properties become constant (not equal) Dynamic, closed-system, fixed-T picture of phase/dissolution equilibria.
Solution
1. (a) True — a physical equilibrium such as liquid⇄vapour needs a closed system at a fixed T so that no component escapes and both phases coexist.
2. (b) True — equilibrium is dynamic: the forward and backward processes keep occurring at equal rates, giving no net change.
3. (c) False — at equilibrium the parameters (concentration, pressure, density) become constant in time; they do not become “equal” to one another. constant ≠ equal
4. (d) True — for a solid dissolving in a liquid, at a fixed temperature the saturation concentration (solubility) is a fixed constant.
5. So the correct statements are (a), (b) and (d); only (c) is wrong.
6. Number of correct statements = 3.
 (a) closed system, fixed T      TRUE
 (b) equal opposing rates        TRUE
 (c) all parameters "equal"      FALSE
       -> they become CONSTANT
 (d) solubility const at fixed T  TRUE
 count = 3                        <-- answer
⚡ JEE Trick “Constant” versus “equal” is the examiner’s trap in (c): at equilibrium the two sides hold steady concentrations that are generally unequal. The other three statements are standard textbook facts, so the count is 3.
Answer: 3.
29A(g) ⇄ 2 B(g) + C(g)
For the given reaction, if the initial pressure is 450 mmHg and the pressure at time t is 720 mmHg at a constant temperature T and constant volume V. The fraction of A(g) decomposed under these conditions is x×10−1. The value of x is (nearest integer) (JEE Main 2023 (10 April Shift 2), Level-3)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2023 · 10 Apr · EveningSolution ↗
Fraction decomposed from pressureA(g)⇌2 B(g)+C(g) For the given reaction, if the initial pressure is 450 mm Hg...🟢 Exact3
Concept At constant volume and temperature, pressure is directly proportional to the number of moles of gas. So the measured rise in total pressure maps directly onto the amount of A that has decomposed, through the mole change Δn of the reaction. No equilibrium constant is required — only a mole balance in pressure units.
Key relation A⇄2B+C: per mole A, gas moles go 1→3 so Δn=+2, giving Ptotal = P0 + 2p p = pressure equivalent of A that has reacted.
Solution
1. Initial pressures: A=450 mmHg, B=C=0. Let p mmHg of A decompose.
2. At time t: A=450−p, B=2p, C=p (from the 1:2:1 stoichiometry).
3. Total = (450−p) + 2p + p = 450 + 2p. net gain 2p per A reacted
4. Set equal to the observed 720: 450 + 2p = 720 ⇒ 2p = 270 ⇒ p = 135 mmHg.
5. Fraction of A decomposed = p/P0 = 135/450 = 0.30.
6. Written as x×10−1: 0.30 = 3×10−1, so x = 3.
7. Sanity: A left = 450−135 = 315 mmHg, and 315+270+135 = 720 ✓, confirming the balance.
 A <=> 2B + C    P0 = 450 mmHg
 decompose p:
   total = 450 + 2p = 720
   2p = 270  ->  p = 135
 fraction = 135/450 = 0.30 = 3e-1  <-- x = 3
⚡ JEE Trick The pressure rise equals Δn×(amount reacted): here Δn=+2, so ΔP=2p=270 gives p at a glance with no Kp at all. The fraction is then p divided by the initial pressure of A (450 mmHg), not by the total pressure — a very common slip that gives a wrong answer.
Answer: 3.
30For reaction: SO2(g) + 1⁄2O2(g) ⇄ SO3(g) Kp = 2×1012 at 27°C and 1 atm pressure. The Kc for the same reaction is ____ ×1013. (Nearest integer) (Given R = 0.082 L atm K−1 mol−1) (JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan. Shift 1), Level-2)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2023 · 31 Jan · MorningSolution ↗
Kc from KpFor reaction : SO2(g) + 1/2 O2(g) ⇌ SO3(g) Kp = 2 × 10^12 at 27°C and 1 atm pressure. Kc ?🟢 Exact1
Concept Kp and Kc are related by Kp=Kc(RT)Δn, where Δn is the change in moles of gas. For SO2+(1/2)O2⇄SO3 the gas moles drop by a half, so Δn=−1/2 and Kc = Kp(RT)+1/2. Because the exponent is only a half, the conversion factor is just a square root.
Key relation Kc = Kp(RT)−Δn = Kp·√(RT) Δn = 1−(1+1/2) = −0.5.
Solution
1. Δngas = moles of product gas − moles of reactant gas = 1 − (1 + 0.5) = −0.5.
2. Rearranging Kp=Kc(RT)Δn gives Kc=Kp(RT)−Δn=Kp(RT)+0.5.
3. T = 27°C = 300 K, so RT = 0.082×300 = 24.6 L atm mol−1. must use kelvin
4. √24.6 ≈ 4.96.
5. Kc = 2×1012 × 4.96 = 9.92×1012.
6. = 0.99×1013 ≈ 1×1013, so the blank (×1013) = 1.
 dn = 1 - (1 + 1/2) = -0.5
 Kc = Kp (RT)^(+0.5)
 RT = 0.082*300 = 24.6
 sqrt(24.6) = 4.96
 Kc = 2e12 * 4.96 = 9.92e12
    = 0.99e13 ~ 1e13              <-- 1
⚡ JEE Trick The sign of Δn decides whether you multiply or divide by (RT)|Δn|; a half-mole Δn means just a square root (≈5), so Kc stays the same order of magnitude as Kp. The answer rounds cleanly to 1.
Answer: 1 (×1013).
31A mixture of one mole of H2O and 1 mole of CO is taken in a 10 litre container and heated to 725 K. At equilibrium 40% of water by mass reacts with carbon monoxide according to the equation:
CO(g) + H2O(g) ⇄ CO2(g) + H2(g). The equilibrium constant KC×102 for the reaction is ____ (Nearest integer) (JEE Main 2023 (11 April Shift 1), Level-2)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2023 · 11 Apr · MorningSolution ↗
Water gas shift KcOne mole of H2O and one mole of CO are taken in 10 L vessel and heated to 725K. At equilibrium 40%🟢 Exact44
Concept The water-gas shift CO+H2O⇄CO2+H2 has equal moles of gas on both sides, so Δn=0 and the vessel volume cancels in Kc — equilibrium mole numbers can be used directly. “40% by mass” of the water is also 40% by moles, since mass and moles are proportional for one substance, giving 0.4 mol reacted.
Key relation Kc = [CO2][H2]/([CO][H2O]) Δn=0 ⇒ volume terms cancel, mole numbers suffice.
Solution
1. Start: 1 mol CO, 1 mol H2O. Water reacted = 40% of 1 = 0.4 mol; CO reacts 1:1, also 0.4 mol.
2. Equilibrium moles: CO = 0.6, H2O = 0.6, CO2 = 0.4, H2 = 0.4.
3. Because Δn = 0, the 10-L volume divides out of numerator and denominator. no need to form concentrations
4. Kc = (0.4×0.4)/(0.6×0.6) = 0.16/0.36.
5. = 0.444.
6. Kc×102 = 44.4 ≈ 44 (nearest integer).
7. Note Kc<1 means the forward (water-gas shift) reaction is only partly complete at 725 K, consistent with just 40% conversion.
 start 1,1 ; 40% H2O reacts = 0.4
 eq: CO=0.6  H2O=0.6  CO2=0.4  H2=0.4
 Kc = (0.4*0.4)/(0.6*0.6)
    = 0.16/0.36 = 0.444
 Kc*100 = 44.4 ~ 44               <-- answer
⚡ JEE Trick “40% by mass” equals 40% by moles for a single substance, giving 0.4 mol immediately. With Δn=0 the 10-L vessel is a red herring — working in moles is faster and avoids any volume slip.
Answer: 44.
324.5 moles each of hydrogen and iodine is heated in a sealed ten litre vessel. At equilibrium, 3 moles of HI were found. The equilibrium constant for H2(g) + I2(g) ⇄ 2HI(g) is ____ (JEE Main 2023 (11 April Shift 2), Level-1)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2023 · 11 Apr · EveningSolution ↗
Kc of HI formation4.5 mol each of hydrogen and iodine heated in a sealed 10 litre vessel. At equilibrium, 3 mol...🟢 Exact1
Concept For H2+I2⇄2HI the moles of gas are equal on both sides, so Δn=0 and the vessel volume cancels in Kc; equilibrium mole numbers are enough. Work backwards from the 3 mol of HI formed to find how much H2 and I2 were consumed, then plug into the Kc expression.
Key relation Kc = [HI]2/([H2][I2]) Forming 2 mol HI consumes 1 mol H2 and 1 mol I2.
Solution
1. HI formed = 3 mol ⇒ H2 used = I2 used = 3/2 = 1.5 mol each.
2. Equilibrium moles: H2 = 4.5 − 1.5 = 3, I2 = 4.5 − 1.5 = 3, HI = 3.
3. Since Δn = 0, the 10-L volume cancels, so mole numbers can be used directly. V0 in both
4. Kc = [HI]2/([H2][I2]) = 32/(3×3).
5. = 9/9 = 1.
6. So the equilibrium constant Kc = 1.
7. Sanity: the fraction of H2 converted is 1.5/4.5 = 1/3, a modest extent, and Kc≈1 indeed means neither direction is strongly favoured.
 start H2 = I2 = 4.5 ; HI formed = 3
 used H2 = I2 = 1.5
 eq: H2 = 3   I2 = 3   HI = 3
 Kc = 3^2/(3*3) = 9/9 = 1         <-- answer
⚡ JEE Trick With Δn=0 the 10-L vessel never enters Kc — use mole numbers straight and skip the concentration step. Equal starting amounts and the tidy equilibrium (all three species at 3 mol) make Kc=1 almost by inspection.
Answer: 1.
332O3(g) ⇄ 3O2(g)
At 300 K, ozone is fifty percent dissociated. The standard free energy change at this temperature and 1 atm pressure is (−)......Jmol−1. (Nearest integer)
[Given: ln1.35 = 0.3 and R = 8.3 J K−1 mol−1] (JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 1), Level-6)
📝 PYQ: JEE Main 2022 · 24 Jun · MorningSolution ↗
Standard free energy from degree of dissociationQ22 JEE Main, Chemistry PYQ June 24, 2022, Shift 1 | 2O3(g)⇌3O2(g) At 300 K, ozone is fifty percent🟢 Exact747
Concept Fifty-percent dissociation of 2O3⇄3O2 at a total pressure of 1 atm fixes the mole fractions, hence the partial pressures and Kp. Then ΔG°=−RTlnKp. The supplied value ln1.35=0.3 is a strong hint that Kp should come out to 1.35, which is a built-in self-check on the mole bookkeeping.
Key relation Kp = pO₂3/pO₃2; ΔG° = −RTlnKp Total P = 1 atm ⇒ each partial pressure equals its mole fraction.
Solution
1. Basis 1 mol O3; α=0.5 dissociates, so O3 consumed = 0.5 mol.
2. By 2O3→3O2: O2 formed = (3/2)×0.5 = 0.75 mol; O3 left = 0.5 mol; total = 1.25 mol.
